Finally, we use
Lemma

41
to show that for arbitrary
\mathsf{p}\in\mathsf{Act}
and
A\subseteq\mathtt{Exp}
we have

\displaystyle\partial(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]})[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times A]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathsf{a}\in A}\partial(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{a})

\displaystyle=\frac{{{r}}}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\sum_{\mathsf{a_{0}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\mathsf{p},\mathsf{a_{0}})

\displaystyle=\frac{{{r}}}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\partial(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times A/{\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{{r}}}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\partial(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times R(A/{\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}})]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{{r}}}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\partial(\mathsf{g}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times R(A)/{\mathsf{g}^{[{{r}}]}}]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{g}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}[\mathsf{p}\times R(A)]

The case of showing that
\partial(\mathsf{g}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times B]\leq\partial(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times R^{-1}(B)]
for arbitrary
B\subseteq\mathtt{Exp}
and
\mathsf{p}\in\mathsf{Act}
is symmetric and is omitted. Therefore
(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]},\mathsf{g}^{[{{r}}]})\in\Phi_{\partial}(R)
.
∎

See
11

Proof.

Observe that the full relation on
\mathtt{Exp}
is a congruence. Since

\Phi_{\partial}
preserves congruences and congruence relations are also preserved by intersections
[
15
, Theorem 5.3]
, by the fixed point characterisation of the greatest fixed point of
\Phi_{\partial}
, we have that the greatest bisimulation on
(\mathtt{Exp},\partial)
is a congruence.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs from
Section

5

Lemma 44
.

Let
\mathsf{e}\in\mathtt{Exp}
and
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
. It holds that,
\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{e}\right)_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)
.

Proof.

By induction on the construction of
\mathsf{e}\in\mathtt{Exp}
.
The base cases hold immediately.
First, consider the case when
\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g}
for some test
{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\in\mathtt{BExp}
. Assume that
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
; then

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g}\right)_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}\right)

(
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

(
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
)

The case when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
is symmetric.

Now, let
\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{r}}}\mathsf{g}
, where
{{r}}\in[0,1]
. We have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{r}}}\mathsf{g}\right)_{\alpha}

\displaystyle={{r}}\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}\right)_{\alpha}+{{(1-r)}}\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{g}\right)_{\alpha}

(Def. of
\mathsf{E}
)

\displaystyle={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

(Def. of
\partial
)

If
\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}
, then we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}\right)_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}\right)_{\alpha}\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{g}\right)_{\alpha}

(Def. of
\mathsf{E}
)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

(Def. of
\partial
)

As for the loops, let’s consider first
\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{f}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})}
for some test
{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\in\mathtt{BExp}
. First, consider the case when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
.
From the definition of
\partial
it immediately follows that

\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})}\right)_{\alpha}={{1}}=\partial(\mathsf{e}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

Again, in the case when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
, it follows from the definition of
\partial
that

\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})}\right)_{\alpha}={{0}}=\partial(\mathsf{f}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

Finally, let’s consider probabilistic loop
\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}
for some
{{r}}={{1}}
.
First, consider the case when
\mathsf{E}\left(f\right)_{\alpha}={{1}}
and
{{r}}={{1}}
.
By the induction hypothesis we have that
\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=1
.
In such a case, by the definition of
\partial
we have that
\partial(\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=0
and therefore

\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\alpha}={{0}}=\partial(\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

For the remaining case we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-r}{1-r\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{f}\right)_{\alpha}}

(Def. of
\mathsf{E}
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-r}{1-r\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

(Def. of
\partial
) ∎

Lemma 45
.

Let
R\subseteq\mathtt{Exp}\times\mathtt{Exp}
be a congruence with respect to
\mathsf{ProbGKAT}
operators such that
(\mathsf{e},\mathsf{f})\in R
, and let
Q\in{\mathtt{Exp}}/{R}
be an equivalence class of
R
. It holds that

Q/\mathsf{e}=Q/\mathsf{f}

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{g}\in Q
.
Since
R
is a congruence and
(\mathsf{e},\mathsf{f})\in R
, we have
(\mathsf{g}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e},\mathsf{g}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})\in R
.
Hence,

\mathsf{g}\in Q/\mathsf{e}\iff\mathsf{g}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}\in Q\iff\mathsf{g}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\in Q\iff\mathsf{g}\in Q/\mathsf{f}\qed

Lemma 46
.

Let
\mathsf{e},\mathsf{f}\in\mathtt{Exp}
and let
Q\in\mathtt{Exp}/{\equiv}
.
Now
(Q/\mathsf{f})/\mathsf{e}=Q/\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{g}\in Q
; we derive as follows

\mathsf{g}\in(Q/\mathsf{f})/\mathsf{e}\iff\mathsf{g}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}\in Q/\mathsf{f}\iff(\mathsf{g}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e})\mathbin{;}{\mathsf{f}}\in Q\iff\mathsf{g}\mathbin{;}(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})\in Q\iff\mathsf{g}\in Q/{\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}}

Here, the second to last step follows by associativity.
∎

Lemma 47
.

Let
(X,\beta)
be an arbitrary
\mathcal{G}
-coalgebra,
x,y\in X
and
R\subseteq X\times X
an equivalence relation on
X
. If for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,
\beta(x)_{\alpha}=\beta(y)_{\alpha}
then
(x,y)\in\Phi_{\beta}(R)

Proof.

Observe that if for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
the distributions
\beta(x)_{\alpha}
and
\beta(y)_{\alpha}
are equal, then conditions of
Lemma

38
immediately hold.
∎

Lemma 48
.

Let
\mathsf{e}\in\mathtt{Exp}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\in\mathtt{BExp}
. If
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
, then
\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})_{\alpha}\lhd_{\alpha}\mathsf{e}
. If
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
, then
\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)={{1}}
. Since
(-\lhd_{\alpha}\mathsf{e})
is a convex extension of the map
c_{\alpha,\mathsf{e}}:\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}+\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp}\to\mathcal{D}_{\omega}(\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}+\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp})
, satisfying that
c_{\alpha,\mathsf{e}}(\checkmark)=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
, we have that

\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})_{\alpha}\lhd_{\alpha}\mathsf{e}={{1}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}\qed

See
12

Proof.

Because of
Lemma

36
, it suffices to show that
{\equiv}\subseteq\Phi_{\partial}(\equiv)
.
We will interchangeably use different characterisations of
\Phi_{\partial}(\equiv)
, including
Definition

9
,
Lemma

38
and
Lemma

47
.

We proceed by induction on the length of derivation of
\equiv
. The equational axioms are base cases, while the quasi-equational axioms are inductive steps.
We need not verify the congruence rules of
\equiv
, since by
Lemma

43
,
\Phi_{\partial}(\equiv)
is also a congruence.

Let
\mathsf{e},\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}\in\mathtt{Exp}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\in\mathtt{BExp}
,
{{v}}\in\mathsf{Out}
and
{{r}},{{s}}\in[0,1]
. The soundness of
guarded choice axioms
,
probabilistic choice axioms
and
distributivity axiom
can be proved using
Lemma

47
by just showing that the each of the distributions given by the derivatives are equal.

(
\mathsf{G1}
)

If
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
, then
\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
.
Similarly, for
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
,
\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
.
Therefore, for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,
\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
.

(
\mathsf{G2}
)

If
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
, then
\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
. By
Lemma

48
we have that
\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
, so
\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}
. If
\alpha\leq\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
, then
\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}

(
\mathsf{G3}
)

If
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
, then
\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{f}+_{\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
.
Similarly, if
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
, then
\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{f}+_{\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
.

(
\mathsf{G4}
)

If
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
and
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}
, then
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{bc}}
, and so

\partial((\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{bc}}}(\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}

Now, let
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
and
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}
. In such a case,
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\overline{bc}}}
and
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}
, and so

\partial((\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{bc}}}(\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}

Finally, when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}}
, we have that
\alpha\leq{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\overline{bc}}}
, and so

\partial((\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{bc}}}(\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}

(
\mathsf{P1}
)

For all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,
\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}

(
\mathsf{P2}
)

For all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,
\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{{1}}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}={{1}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}+{{0}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}

(
\mathsf{P3}
)

For all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,

\displaystyle\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}

\displaystyle={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}

\displaystyle={{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}+{{(1-(1-r))}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{1-r}}}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}

(
\mathsf{P4}
)

Recall that by assumption
{{rs}}\neq{{1}}
. For all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,

\displaystyle\partial((\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f})\oplus_{{{s}}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}

\displaystyle={{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}+{{(1-r)s}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}+{{(1-s)}}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}

\displaystyle={{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}+{{(1-rs)}}\left({{\frac{(1-r)s}{1-rs}}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}+{{\frac{1-s}{1-rs}}}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}\right)

Let
{{t}}={{\frac{(1-r)s}{1-rs}}}
. Hence, the previous is equal to

{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}+{{(1-rs)}}\left({{t}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}+{{(1-t)}}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}\right)=\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{{rs}}}(\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{t}}}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}

(
\mathsf{D}
)

For
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
, we have

\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}(\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}=\partial((\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})\oplus_{{r}}(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}

Similarly, when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
, we have

\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}(\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}=\partial((\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})\oplus_{{r}}(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}

As for the
sequencing axioms
, we will use characterisations of
\Phi_{\partial}(\equiv)
both from
Lemma

38
and
Lemma

47
depending on the case.

(S1)

Since for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
we have that
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}
, we can immediately use
Lemma

48
to show that
\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}
. By
Lemma

47
we have that
({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e},\mathsf{e})\in\Phi_{\partial}(\equiv)

(S2)

We verify the conditions of
Lemma

38
. Let
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
.

1.

For all
o\in\{\hbox to5.54pt{\vbox to5.54pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.34999pt\lower-0.34999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\}+\mathsf{Out}
, we have that

\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}(o)=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}(o)=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(o)

In the remaining case, we have that

\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

2.

Let
\mathsf{p}\in\mathsf{Act}
and
Q\in\mathtt{Exp}/\equiv
be an equivalence class of
\equiv
. By
Lemma

42
we have that

\displaystyle\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}]+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}[\{p\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}]

Observe that since
Q
is an equivalence class of the relation
\equiv
, we have that

\mathsf{h}\in Q/{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\iff\mathsf{h}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\in Q\iff\mathsf{h}\in Q

Since
Q=Q/{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}
, we have that

\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

(S3)

We verify the conditions of
Lemma

38
. Let
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
.

1.

For all
o\in\{\hbox to5.54pt{\vbox to5.54pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.34999pt\lower-0.34999pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 C 2.33 2.88 3.81 4.36 6.69 6.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.34 6.36 C 2.79 3.92 3.66 2.61 5.35 0.33}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\}+\mathsf{Out}
, we have that

\displaystyle\partial((\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}(o)

In the remaining case, we have that

\partial((\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

2.

Let
\mathsf{p}\in\mathsf{Act}
and
Q\in\mathtt{Exp}/\equiv
. By
Lemma

42
and
Lemma

46
,

\displaystyle\partial((\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{g}]+\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times(Q/\mathsf{f})/\mathsf{g}]+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{g}]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}{}+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{p\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}]+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}))_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

All the remaining elements of
\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}+\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp}
have no probability mass assigned by those distributions. Hence, for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,
\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}=\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}})_{\alpha}
. Therefore, we can use

47
to obtain the desired result.

. We have that

\displaystyle\partial((\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

For the remaining case, we have

\displaystyle\partial((\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

2.

\mathsf{p}\in\mathsf{Act}
and
Q\in\mathtt{Exp}/\equiv
. By
Lemma

42
we have that

\displaystyle\partial((\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{g}]+\partial(\mathsf{e}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(g)_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{g}]+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(g)_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

We omit the symmetric cases when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
.

, we have that

\displaystyle\partial((\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f})\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f})(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})(o)

\displaystyle={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(o)+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}{}+\left({{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\right)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}g)_{\alpha}(o)+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

\displaystyle={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

\displaystyle={{r}}\left(\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times{Q}/\mathsf{g}]+\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+{{(1-r)}}\left(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times{Q}/\mathsf{g}]+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]\right)

\displaystyle={{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]+{{(1-r)}}\partial(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g}\oplus_{{{r}}}\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

(
\mathsf{S8}
)

We use
Lemma

47
and show the equality of distributions for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
. First, consider the case when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
and
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}
. Observe, that it is equivalent to
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{bc}}
. In such a case, we have that

\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)={{1}}=\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{bc}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

Since both distributions assign all the probability mass to the same element, they are equal. Now, consider the case when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
. In such a case, this implies that
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{bc}}}
. We have that

Now, let’s consider the
loop axioms
. All cases will rely on
Lemma

38
.

(
\mathsf{L1}
)

First, consider the situation when
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}
. Then,

\partial\left(\mathsf{e}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})}\right)_{\alpha}(\checkmark)={{1}}=\partial({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=\partial\left(\mathsf{e}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\right)_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

Since both distributions assign all the probability mass to
\checkmark
, all conditions of
Lemma

38
are immediately satisfied. For the rest of cases we will assume that
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}
. First consider the subcase when
\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)={{1}}
. Then,

which is enough to verify the conditions of

.For the remainder of this case, we can safely assume that

and

\mathsf{L5}

)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{r}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-\partial((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{r}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{r}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{r}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)\left(\frac{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)\left({{1}}+\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)}

\displaystyle=\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\frac{{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{(1-r)}}}

. We have that

\displaystyle\partial\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\alpha}(o)=\partial\left(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\alpha}(o)

(

48
)

\displaystyle=\frac{{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{r}}\partial((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{s}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{s}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}

\displaystyle={{t}}\left(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\frac{{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}\right)

\displaystyle={{t}}\left(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(o)+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\frac{{{r}}\partial((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{s}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(o)}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{s}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathbin{;}(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}{\mathsf{e}}^{[{{r}}]}\oplus_{{{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})\right)_{\alpha}(o)

Lemma

)

As for the probability of outputting
\checkmark
, we have that

\displaystyle\partial\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\alpha}(\checkmark)=\partial\left(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\alpha}(\checkmark)

(

)

\displaystyle=\frac{{{1-r}}}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle=\frac{{{1-r}}}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{(1-r)}}\left({{t}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{t}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)+{{1}}\right)}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{(1-r)}}\left({{t}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{\frac{rs}{1-r(1-s)}}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)+{{1}}\right)}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{(1-r)}}{{t}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{\frac{1-r}{1-r(1-s)}}}{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)+{{(1-r(1-s))\frac{1-r}{1-r(1-s)}}}}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{t}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark){{(1-r)}}+{{\frac{1-r}{1-r(1-s)}}}\left({{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}\right)}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}

\displaystyle={{t}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\frac{{{1-r}}}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}+{{\frac{1-r}{1-r(1-s)}}}

\displaystyle={{\frac{rs}{1-r(1-s)}}}\left(\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]{{\left(1-r(1-s)\right)}}}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}\right)

\displaystyle={{t}}\left(\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]{{\left(1-r(1-s)\right)}}+{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 46.2497pt-t\frac{{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}

\displaystyle={{t}}\left(\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]\left({{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}\right)+{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}\right)

\displaystyle={{t}}\left(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\frac{{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]}{{{1}}-{{rs}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)-{{r(1-s)}}}\right)

\displaystyle={{t}}\left(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\frac{{{r}}\partial((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{s}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{s}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\right)

\displaystyle={{t}}\left(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\frac{{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{e})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)}\right)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle={{t}}\left(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q/\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}]+\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)\partial\left(\mathsf{e}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]\right)

\displaystyle=\partial\left(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}{\mathsf{e}}^{[{{r}}]}\oplus_{{{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\right)_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\partial\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathbin{;}(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}{\mathsf{e}}^{[{{r}}]}\oplus_{{{t}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})\right)_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{p}\}\times Q]

) ∎

Remark 49
.

We can substitute
\mathsf{e}
in the consequence of
\mathsf{L5}
, to obtain

{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathbin{;}((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{s}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{1}}+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}}\mathsf{g}))^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathbin{;}(\mathsf{f}\mathbin{;}((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{s}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{1}})+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}}\mathsf{g})^{({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}})}+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{1}})

which is equivalent to
\mathsf{L5}
by congruence.
A similar translation turns
\mathsf{L6}
into an equational axiom.
Alternatively, one could replace
\mathsf{L5}
and
\mathsf{L6}
with the following quasi-equational axiom, which can be proved to imply both:

\frac{\mathsf{e}\equiv((\mathsf{f}\oplus_{s}\mathsf{e})\oplus_{{{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{1}})+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}}\mathsf{g}\quad{{r}}(1-{{s}})\neq 1}{\mathsf{e}\equiv(\mathsf{f}\oplus_{\frac{{{r}}{{s}}}{1-{{r}}(1-{{s}})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{1}})+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}}\mathsf{g}}

This rule cannot be replaced by an axiom in the same way that
\mathsf{L5}
and
\mathsf{L6}
can, on account of the recurrence of
\mathsf{e}
in the premise.

D.2
Generalised guarded and convex sums

We now rigorously define our generalised sum operators, and verify that they expect as behaved.
First off is the guarded sum.

Definition 51
.

Consider a subset
\Phi\subseteq\mathsf{At}
and a collection
\{\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Phi}
such that for all
\alpha\in\Phi
,
\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\in\mathtt{Exp}
. A
generalised guarded sum
is an expression inductively given by the following

\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}=\mathsf{e}_{\gamma}+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\quad\text{if }\gamma\in\Phi\quad\quad\quad\quad\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}={\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\quad\text{if }\Phi=\emptyset

This definition is ambiguous in the choice of expressions from the collection when unrolling the inductive definition.
However, all possible ways of doing so are equivalent.

Lemma 52

(
[
64
, Lemma B.1]
)
.

Generalised guarded sums are well defined up to
\equiv

We first recall properties of generalised guarded sums from
[
64
]
.

Lemma 53

(
[
64
, Lemma B.2]
)
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\in\mathtt{BExp}
and let
\{\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}
be an indexed collection such that
\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\in\mathtt{Exp}
for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
. Then,

{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\mathbin{;}\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{bc}}}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}

Observe that in the above lemma, similarly to
[
64
]
we abuse notation and write
\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}
for the set
\{\alpha\mid\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\}
. Since the set
\mathsf{T}
is finite, the Boolean algebra generated by it is atomic and hence every
{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{c}}\in\mathtt{BExp}
corresponds to the subset of
\mathsf{At}
.

Lemma 54

(
[
64
, Lemma B.4]
)
.

Let
\Phi\subseteq\mathsf{At}
and
\{\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Phi}
be an indexed collection such that
\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\in\mathtt{Exp}
for each
\alpha\in\Phi
. Then,

\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\alpha}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}

Now, we state further properties of generalised guarded sums, which will be used in the proof of
Theorem

14
.

Lemma 55
.

Let
\Phi\subseteq\mathsf{At}
and
\{\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Phi}
be an indexed collection such that
\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\in\mathtt{Exp}
for each
\alpha\in\Phi
and let
\mathsf{f}\in\mathtt{Exp}
. Then,

\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

Proof.

By induction on the size of
\Phi
. For the base case, when
\Phi=\emptyset
consider the following.

\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

For the inductive step, we have that

\displaystyle\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\mathsf{e}_{\gamma}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

(
\Phi
is nonempty)

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\mathsf{e}_{\gamma}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\right)

(
\mathsf{S5}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{\gamma}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\right)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\qed

Lemma 56
.

Let
\Phi\subseteq\mathsf{At}
and let
\{\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Phi}
and
\{\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Phi}
be indexed collections such that
\mathsf{e}_{\alpha},\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\in\mathtt{Exp}
for each
\alpha\in\Psi
and let
{{r}}\in[0,1]
. Then,

\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\oplus_{{{r}}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\right)\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\left(\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\oplus_{{{r}}}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\right)

Proof.

By induction on the size of
\Phi
. For the base case, consider the following.

\displaystyle\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\oplus_{{r}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\oplus_{{r}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\left(\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\oplus_{{{r}}}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\right)

For the inductive step, we have that

\displaystyle\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\oplus_{{r}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\mathsf{e}_{\gamma}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\oplus_{{r}}\left(\mathsf{f}_{\gamma}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv(\mathsf{e}_{\gamma}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f}_{\gamma})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\left(\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\right)\oplus_{{r}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\right)\right)

(
\mathsf{DF9}
)

\displaystyle\equiv(\mathsf{e}_{\gamma}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f}_{\gamma})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\setminus\{\gamma\}}(\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha})\right)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi}(\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\oplus_{{r}}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha})

∎

Remark 57
.

In terms of notation, given
\Phi,\Psi\subseteq\mathsf{At}
such that
\Psi\cap\Theta=\emptyset
and indexed collections
\{\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Psi}
,
\{\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\Phi}
such that
\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\in\mathtt{Exp}
for each
\alpha\in\Phi
and
\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}
for each
\alpha\in\Psi
we will write

\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Psi}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}\;{{+}}\;\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Theta}\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}

to denote the following

\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\Phi\cup\Psi}\mathsf{g}_{\alpha}\quad\quad\quad\text{where}\quad\quad\mathsf{g}_{\alpha}=\begin{cases}\mathsf{e}_{\alpha}&\text{if }\alpha\in\Phi\\
\mathsf{f}_{\alpha}&\text{if }\alpha\in\Psi\\
\end{cases}

Next up is the generalised convex sum.

Definition 58
.

Given a non-empty finite index set
I
and indexed collections
\{{{r}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
such that
\mathsf{e}_{i}\in\mathtt{Exp}
and
{{r}}_{i}\in[0,1]
for all
i\in I
satisfying that
\sum_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}={{1}}
we define a
generalised convex sum
to be an expression given by the following inductive definition

\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{e}_{j}\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}{\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{j}}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\quad\text{for }j\in I\text{ and }{{r}}_{j}\neq{{1}}

\bigoplus_{i\in\{j\}}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{e}_{j}\quad\text{otherwise}

Again, the above definition is ambiguous in the choice of expressions from the collection, however all possible unrollings of the definition are provably equivalent up to
\equiv
.

Lemma 59
.

Generalised convex sums are well defined up to
\equiv

Proof.

Let
I
be a finite and non-empty index set,
\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{{{r}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
indexed collections such that for all
i\in I
we have that
\mathsf{e}_{i}\in\mathtt{Exp}
and
{{r}}_{i}\in[0,1]
.

We show that generalised sums are well-defined by induction on the size of the index set
I
. The case when
I=\{j\}
trivial, as in such a case the generalised sum is defined to be just
\mathsf{e}_{j}
.

When
I=\{j,k\}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}_{j}\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in\{k\}}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{j}\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}}\mathsf{e}_{k}\equiv\mathsf{e}_{k}\oplus_{{{1}}-{{r}}_{j}}\mathsf{e}_{j}

(
\mathsf{P3}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{k}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}}\mathsf{e}_{j}

(
{{r}}_{j}+{{r}}_{k}={{1}}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{k}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in\{j\}}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

For the induction step, assume that
|I|>2
and that
j,k\in I
. Consider the following

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}_{j}\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{j}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\equiv\mathsf{e}_{j}\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}}\left(\mathsf{e}_{k}\oplus_{\frac{{{r}}_{k}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{j}}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j,k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{({{1}}-{{r}}_{j})(1-{{r}}_{k})}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\mathsf{e}_{j}\oplus_{\frac{{{r}}_{j}}{{{r}}_{j}+{{r}}_{k}}}\mathsf{e}_{k}\right)\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}+{{r}}_{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j,k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{({{1}}-{{r}}_{j})(1-{{r}}_{k})}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

(
\mathsf{DF11}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\mathsf{e}_{k}\oplus_{\frac{{{r}}_{k}}{{{r}}_{j}+{{r}}_{k}}}\mathsf{e}_{j}\right)\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}+{{r}}_{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j,k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{({{1}}-{{r}}_{j})(1-{{r}}_{k})}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

(
\mathsf{P3}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{k}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}}\left(\mathsf{e}_{j}\oplus_{\frac{{{r}}_{j}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{k}}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j,k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{({{1}}-{{r}}_{j})(1-{{r}}_{k})}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\right)

(
\mathsf{P4}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{k}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{k}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\qed

Remark 60
.

Notation wise, given non-empty finite index sets
I
and
J
and collections
\{{{r}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{{{s}}_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of probabilities as well as collections
\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{\mathsf{f}_{j}\}_{j\in J}
of expressions satisfying
\sum_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}+\sum_{j\in J}{{s}}_{j}={{1}}
and
I\cap J=\emptyset
we will write

\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\;{{\oplus}}\;\bigoplus_{j\in I}{{s}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{i}

to denote the generalised convex sum

\bigoplus_{k\in I\cup J}{{t}}_{k}\cdot\mathsf{g}_{k}\quad\quad\text{where}\quad{{t}}_{k}=\begin{cases}{{r}}_{k}&\text{if }k\in I\\
{{s}}_{k}&\text{if }k\in J\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{g}_{k}=\begin{cases}\mathsf{e}_{k}&\text{if }k\in I\\
\mathsf{f}_{k}&\text{if }k\in J\end{cases}

In the case of singleton index sets
I=\{j\}
and collections
\{{{r}}_{i}\}_{i\in I},\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
we will simply write
{{r}}_{j}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{j}
instead of
\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}

Lemma 61
.

Let
I
be a non-empty finite index set,
\{{{r}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
indexed collections such that
{{r}}_{i}\in[0,1]
and
\mathsf{e}_{i}\in\mathtt{Exp}
for all
i\in I
and let
\mathsf{f}\in\mathtt{Exp}
. Then,

\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\equiv\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

Proof.

By induction on the size of index set
I
. For the base case, when
I=\{j\}
we have that

\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\equiv\mathsf{e_{j}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\equiv\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

For the induction step, consider the following

\displaystyle\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\mathsf{e}_{j}\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{j}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

(
j\in I
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{j}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{j}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

(
\mathsf{S6}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{j}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\oplus_{{{r}}_{j}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{j\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{j}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}\right)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle\equiv\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}

∎

Lemma 62
.

Let
I
be a non-empty index set,
\{{{r}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}_{i_{I}}
indexed collections such that for all
i\in I
,
{{i}}\in[0,1]
and
\mathsf{e}_{i}\in\mathtt{Exp}
. Let
\mathsf{e}
be an expression, such that
\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{e}_{k}
for some
k\in I
. It holds that

\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\equiv\bigoplus_{j\in J}{{s}}_{j}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{j}

where
J=\{\mathsf{e}\}\cup\{i\in I\mid\mathsf{e}_{i}\neq\mathsf{e}\}
,
\{\mathsf{f}_{j}\}_{j\in J}=\begin{cases}\mathsf{e}&\text{if }j=\mathsf{e}\\
\mathsf{e}_{i}&\text{if }j=i\end{cases}
and
\{s_{j}\}_{j\in J}=\begin{cases}\sum_{\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{e}}{{r}}_{i}&\text{if }j=\mathsf{e}\\
{{r}}_{i}&\text{if }j=i\end{cases}

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that
I=\{1,\dots,n\}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
and because of
Lemma

59
we can safely assume that there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
i\in I
, such that
i\leq k
we have that
\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{e}
and for all
i\in I
such that
i>k
it holds that
\mathsf{e}_{i}\neq\mathsf{e}
.

We proceed by the induction on
k
. If
k=1
, then the desired property holds immediately. For the induction step, consider the following.

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}_{k}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{k}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

(
k\in I
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-{{r}}_{k}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

(
\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{e}_{k}
)

In the inductive step, we will use the following shorthand

{{n}}=\sum_{\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{e}}^{i<k}{{{r}}_{i}}\quad\quad{{m}}=\frac{{{n}}}{1-{{r}}_{k}}

Using the induction hypothesis, we can rewrite the above expression as

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}\oplus{{{r}}_{k}}\left(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{m}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{\mathsf{e},k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{({{1}}-{{r}}_{k}){{(1-m)}}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\equiv\left(\mathsf{e}\oplus_{\frac{{{r}}_{k}}{{{r}}_{k}+{{m}}}}\mathsf{e}\right)\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}+{{n}}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{\mathsf{e},k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{({{1}}-{{r}}_{k}){{(1-m)}}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

(
\mathsf{DF11}
)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\equiv\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}+{{n}}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{\mathsf{e},k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{({{1}}-{{r}}_{k}){{(1-m)}}}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

(
\mathsf{P1}
)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\equiv\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}+{{n}}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I\setminus\{\mathsf{e},k\}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1}}-({{r}}_{k}+{{n}})}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\equiv\mathsf{e}\oplus_{{{r}}_{k}+{{n}}}\left(\bigoplus_{j\in J\setminus\{\mathsf{e}\}}\frac{{{s}}_{j}}{{{1}}-({{r}}_{k}+{{n}})}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{j}\right)\equiv\bigoplus_{j\in J}{{s}}_{j}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{j}

∎

Lemma 63
.

Let
I
be a non-empty finite index set,
\{{{r}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
indexed collections such that
{{r}}_{i}\in[0,1]
and
\mathsf{e}_{i}\in\mathtt{Exp}
for all
i\in I
. Let
E=\bigcup_{i\in I}\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}
. Then,

\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\equiv\bigoplus_{\mathsf{e}\in E}\left(\sum_{\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{e}}{{r}}_{i}\right)\cdot\mathsf{e}

Proof.

By induction on the size of
E
. In the base case, when
E
is a singleton, the result holds immediately by
Lemma

62
. For the induction step, consider the following. Let
\mathsf{g}\in E
be an arbitrary expression and let
J=\{\mathsf{g}\}\cup\{i\in I\mid\mathsf{e}_{i}\neq\mathsf{g}\}
,

{{m}}=\sum_{\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{g}}{{r}}_{i}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\{\mathsf{f}_{j}\}_{j\in J}=\begin{cases}\mathsf{e}&\text{if }j=\mathsf{g}\\
\mathsf{e}_{i}&\text{if }j=i\end{cases}\quad\quad\text{and}\quad\quad\{s_{j}\}_{j\in J}=\begin{cases}{{m}}&\text{if }j=\mathsf{g}\\
{{r}}_{i}&\text{if }j=i\end{cases}

We now have that

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv\bigoplus_{j\in J}{{s}}_{j}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{j}

(
Lemma

62
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{g}\oplus_{{{m}}}\left(\bigoplus_{j\in J\setminus\{\mathsf{g}\}}\frac{{{s}}_{j}}{{{1-m}}}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{g}\oplus_{{{m}}}\left(\bigoplus_{\mathsf{e}\in E\setminus\{\mathsf{g}\}}\left(\sum_{\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{e}}\frac{{{r}}_{i}}{{{1-m}}}\right)\cdot\mathsf{e}\right)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle\equiv\bigoplus_{\mathsf{e}\in E}\left(\sum_{\mathsf{e}_{i}=\mathsf{e}}{{r}}_{i}\right)\cdot\mathsf{e}

∎

Lemma 64
.

Let
I
be a non-empty finite index set,
\{{{r}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and
\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
indexed collections such that
{{r}}_{i}\in[0,1]
and
\mathsf{e}_{i}\in\mathtt{Exp}
for all
i\in I
. Let
E=\bigcup_{i\in I}\{\mathsf{e}_{i}\}
. Then,

\bigoplus_{i\in I}{{r}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{e}_{i}\equiv\bigoplus_{[\mathsf{e}]_{\equiv}\in E/\equiv}\left(\sum_{\mathsf{e}_{i}\equiv\mathsf{e}}{{r}}_{i}\right)\cdot\mathsf{e}

Proof.

A similar result to
Lemma

62
can be obtained when replacing the strict equality with
\equiv
. Using it, one can show the desired result by adapting
Lemma

63
.
∎

D.3
Fundamental Theorem

See
14

Proof.

We proceed by structural induction on the construction of
\mathsf{e}\in\mathtt{Exp}
. The base cases are trivial, hence we only show one of them.

\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{a}

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}

\displaystyle\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\mathsf{a}

(
\mathsf{G1}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\mathsf{a}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}

(
\mathsf{S2}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{a})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{a})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

(Def. of
\partial
)

Now, we can move on to inductive steps.

\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g}

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{f}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{g}

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 46.2497pt+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

(Ind. hypothesis)

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 46.2497pt+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}\mathbin{;}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

(
\mathsf{G3}
and
\mathsf{G2}
)

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 46.2497pt+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}\mathbin{;}\bar{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}\mathbin{;}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{g})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

(Boolean algebra and
\mathsf{S8}
)

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}\mathbin{;}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\leq_{\mathsf{BA}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{b}}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)={{1}}
. In such a case, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}

\displaystyle\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial\left(\mathsf{f}\right)_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

(Ind. hypothesis)

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}\setminus\{\gamma\}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial\left(\mathsf{f}\right)_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

\displaystyle\equiv({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\oplus_{{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}\setminus\{\gamma\}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial\left(\mathsf{f}\right)_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

(
\mathsf{P2}
)

\displaystyle\equiv({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\oplus_{{o}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}\setminus\{\gamma\}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial\left(\mathsf{f}\right)_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

(
\mathsf{P3}
)

Now, we can apply the axiom
\mathsf{L6}
and obtain the following

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\mathbin{;}({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}\oplus_{{0}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}})

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\mathbin{;}({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\oplus_{{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{0}}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]})

(
\mathsf{P3}
)

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\mathbin{;}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}

(
\mathsf{P2}
)

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\mathbin{;}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}\left(\partial\left(\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\gamma}\right)}\partial\left(\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}\right)_{\gamma}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

(Def. of
\partial
)

Now, consider the case of
\gamma\in\mathsf{At}
, such that
\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)<{{1}}
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}

\displaystyle\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

(Induction hypothesis)

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}\setminus\{\gamma\}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\left({\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\oplus_{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma})\setminus\{\checkmark\}}\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(d)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)}\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}\setminus\{\gamma\}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma})\setminus\{\checkmark\}}\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(d)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)}\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\oplus_{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+_{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\left(\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}\setminus\{\gamma\}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha})}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\right)

(
\mathsf{P3}
)

We can now apply
\mathsf{L5}
and obtain the following

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\mathbin{;}\left(\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma})\setminus\{\checkmark\}}\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(d)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)}\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}\oplus_{\frac{{{r(}}{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark))}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\gamma}}\mathbin{;}\left(\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma})\setminus\{\checkmark\}}\frac{\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(d)}{{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)}\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\mathbin{;}\mathsf{f}^{[{{r}}]}\right)\oplus_{\frac{{{r(}}{{1}}-\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark))}{{{1}}-{{r}}\partial(\mathsf{f})_{\gamma}(\checkmark)}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.44,0.57}\mathsf{\mathsf{1}}}\right)

(
Lemma

61
)

We can unroll the convex sum and apply the

Proofs from

6

To prove

21
, we use the following.

Lemma 65

(
[
57

)
.

Let
(X,\beta)
be a
\mathsf{ProbGKAT}
automaton,
R\subseteq X\times X
a bisimulation equivalence on
(X,\beta)

the canonical quotient map. Then, there exists a unique transition map

which makes

[-]_{R}
into a
\mathsf{ProbGKAT}
automaton homomorphism from
(X,\beta)
to
(X/R,\bar{\beta})
.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\qquad\bar{\partial}\circ[-]_{\equiv}\circ h=\mathcal{G}([-]_{\equiv}\circ h)\circ\beta

(1)

Now, since
[-]_{\equiv}
is a coalgebra homomorphism,
\bar{\partial}\circ[-]_{\equiv}=G[-]_{\equiv}\circ\partial
.
This tells us that (
1
) is equivalent to

\mathcal{G}[-]_{\equiv}\circ\partial\circ h=\mathcal{G}([-]_{\equiv}\circ h)\circ\beta

We can spell out the above equation in the more concrete terms: for all
x\in X
and
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,

1.

\beta(x)_{\alpha}(o)=\partial(h(x))_{\alpha}(o)
for any
o\in\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}
, and

2.

for any
(\mathsf{a},[\mathsf{e^{\prime}}])\in\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp}/{\equiv}
,

\sum_{h(x^{\prime})\equiv\mathsf{e^{\prime}}}\beta(x)_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},x^{\prime})=\sum_{\mathsf{f}\equiv\mathsf{e^{\prime}}}\partial(h(x))_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{f})

Going from right to left, assume that
[-]_{\equiv}\circ h
is a homomorphism.
Let
(X,\tau)
be the Salomaa system associated with
(X,\beta)
, and let
h^{\#}
be the unique extension of
h
to expressions in
\mathtt{Exp}(X)
.
We want to show that
h(x)\equiv(h^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)
for all
x\in X
.
By
Theorem

14
we have that for all
x\in X
,

h(x)\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(h(x))_{\alpha})}\partial(h)_{\alpha}(d)\cdot\mathtt{exp}(d)\right)

We can unroll the convex sum and use
Lemma

64
to group together elements of the support which are equivalent up to
\equiv
. We obtain the following for all
x\in X

h(x)\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}}\partial(h(x))_{\alpha}(d)\cdot d\;{{\oplus}}\;\bigoplus_{(a,[\mathsf{e^{\prime}}]_{\equiv})\in E_{\alpha}}\left(\sum_{\mathsf{f}\equiv\mathsf{e^{\prime}}}\partial(h(x))_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{f})\right)\cdot\mathsf{a}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e^{\prime}}\right)

(2)

where for each
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
we have that

E_{\alpha}=\{(\mathsf{a},Q)\in\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp}/{\equiv}\mid\mathsf{e^{\prime}}\in Q,\partial(h(x))_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{e^{\prime}})>{{0}}\}

Similarly, the right hand side of the equation can written as the following for all
x\in X

(h^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}}\beta(x)_{\alpha}(d)\cdot d\;{{\oplus}}\;\bigoplus_{(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{f})\in\mathtt{supp}(\beta(x)_{\alpha})}\beta(x)_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},{f})\cdot\mathsf{a}\mathbin{;}h({f})\right)

We use
Lemma

64
to group together elements of the support which are equivalent up to
\equiv
and sum their associated probabilities

(h^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}}\beta(x)_{\alpha}(d)\cdot d\;{{\oplus}}\;\bigoplus_{(\mathsf{a},[\mathsf{e^{\prime}}]_{\equiv})\in F_{\alpha}}\left(\sum_{h(x^{\prime})\equiv\mathsf{e^{\prime}}}\beta(x)_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},x^{\prime})\right)\cdot\mathsf{a}\mathbin{;}\mathsf{e^{\prime}}\right)

(3)

where for each
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
we define

F_{\alpha}=\{(\mathsf{a},Q)\in\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp}/{\equiv}\mid h(x^{\prime})\in Q,\beta(x)_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},x^{\prime})>{{0}}\}

We show that for each
x\in X
, the expressions from (
2
) and (
3
) are precisely the same.
We do this by arguing that for each atom
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
, the index sets of generalised convex sums are the same and so are the probabilities associated with equivalence classes of elements of both index sets.

Fix an arbitrary
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
.
Given
d\in\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}
, if
d\in\mathtt{supp}(\partial(h(x)))_{\alpha}
, then because of condition (1) of being a homomorphism,
d\in\mathtt{supp}(\beta(x)_{\alpha})
.
The converse holds via a symmetric argument.
Moreover, for all such
d
, we have that
\partial(h(x))_{\alpha}(d)=\beta(x)_{\alpha}(d)
.

Now consider
(\mathsf{a},Q)\in\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp}/{\equiv}
.
If
(a,Q)\in E_{\alpha}
, then there exists some
\mathsf{e^{\prime}}\in\mathtt{Exp}
such that
\partial(h(x))_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{e^{\prime}})>{{0}}
.
Because of condition (2) of being a homomorphism, there exists some
x^{\prime}\in X
satisfying
h(x^{\prime})\equiv\mathsf{e^{\prime}}
and
\beta(x)_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},x^{\prime})>{{0}}
, hence
(\mathsf{a},Q)\in F_{\alpha}
.
The converse follows via a symmetric argument and therefore
E_{\alpha}=F_{\alpha}
.
Observe that because of condition (2) the probabilities associated with each
(\mathsf{a},Q)\in\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp}/{\equiv}
are the same.

Since all generalised convex sums in both expressions assign the same probabilities to the same elements, we obtain
h(x)\equiv(h^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)
for all
x\in X
as desired.

Left to right, assume that
h
is a solution up to
\equiv
.
We first show that
(\partial\circ h^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)=(\mathcal{G}(h)\circ\beta)(x)
for all
x\in X
.
Recall that for all
x\in X
,

(h^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)\equiv\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{d\in\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}}\beta(x)_{\alpha}(d)\cdot d\;{{\oplus}}\;\bigoplus_{(\mathsf{a},{f})\in\mathtt{supp}(\beta(x)_{\alpha})}\beta(x)_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},{f})\cdot\mathsf{a}\mathbin{;}h({f})\right)

Applying the transition map to that expression can be split into two cases for all
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
.

1.

For
o\in\mathsf{2}+\mathsf{Out}
,
(\partial\circ h^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)(o)=\beta(x)_{\alpha}(o)=(\mathcal{G}(h)\circ\beta)(x)_{\alpha}(o)

2.

For all
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{e^{\prime}})\in\mathsf{Act}\times\mathtt{Exp}
,

(\partial\circ h^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{e^{\prime}})=\sum_{h(x^{\prime})=\mathsf{e^{\prime}}}\beta(x)_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},x^{\prime})=(\mathcal{G}(h)\circ\beta)(x)_{\alpha}(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{e^{\prime}})

which proves that
(\partial\circ h^{\#}\circ\tau)=(\mathcal{G}(h)\circ\beta)
.
Now postcompose both sides with
G[-]_{\equiv}
and consider the following

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[-]_{\equiv}\circ\partial\circ h^{\#}\circ\tau

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}[-]_{\equiv}\circ\mathcal{G}(h)\circ\beta

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}[-]_{\equiv}\circ\partial\circ h^{\#}\circ\tau

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}([-]_{\equiv}\circ h)\circ\beta

(
\mathcal{G}
is a functor)

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}\circ[-]_{\equiv}\circ h^{\#}\circ\tau

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}([-]_{\equiv}\circ h)\circ\beta

(
[-]_{\equiv}
is a homomorphism)

\displaystyle\bar{\partial}\circ[-]_{\equiv}\circ h

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}([-]_{\equiv}\circ h)\circ\beta

(
h\equiv h^{\#}\circ\tau
)

And therefore
[-]_{\equiv}\circ h
is a
\mathcal{G}
-coalgebra homomorphism from
(X,\beta)
to
(\mathtt{Exp}/{\equiv},\bar{\partial})
as desired.
∎

Proof.

d(x,x)=0
holds immediately since the greatest bisimulation on
(X,\beta)
is an equivalence relation and therefore for all
x\in X
we have that
x\sim x
.

To show symmetry, assume
d(x,y)=k
for some
k\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
If
k=0
then
x\sim_{\beta}y
, but since the greatest bisimulation on
(X,\beta)
is an equivalence relation (by preservation of weak pullbacks for
G
), we have that
y\sim_{\beta}x
, which implies that
d(y,x)=0
.
For the remaining case, we can assume without loss of generality that
k=2^{-n}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
In such a case,
x\sim_{\beta}^{(n)}y
, but again by
Lemma

38
, each of the stratified bisimilarity relations are equivalence relations.
Therefore,
y\in\sim_{\beta}^{(n)}x
and
n
is the largest number such that
y\in\sim_{\beta}^{(n)}x
.
It follows that
d(y,x)=2^{-n}=k=d(x,y)
as desired.

For the strengthened triangle inequality, first consider the case where
d(x,y)=2^{-k}
and
d(y,z)=2^{-l}
.
These imply
x\in\sim_{\beta}^{(k)}y
and
y\in\sim_{\beta}^{(l)}z
respectively.
We have that
(x,y),(y,z)\in{\sim_{\beta}^{(\min(k,l))}}
.
Since all the stratified bisimilarity relations are equivalence relations, it follows from transitivity that
x\in{\sim_{\beta}^{(\min(k,l))}}z
and
n=\min(k,l)
is the largest natural number for which
x\in\sim_{\beta}^{(n)}z
.
Therefore,
d(x,z)=2^{-\min(k,l)}=\max(2^{-k},2^{-l})=\max(d(x,y),d(y,z))
.

In case
d(x,y)=d(y,z)=0
, we have
x\sim_{\beta}y
and
y\sim_{\beta}z
.
Since the greatest bisimulation is an equivalence relation, we have that
x\sim_{\beta}z
, which implies that
d(x,y)=0=\max(d(x,y),d(y,z))
.

For the next case, let
d(x,y)=0
and
d(y,z)=2^{-k}
.
Then
x\sim_{\beta}y
and
(y,z)\in\sim_{\beta}^{(k)}
.
In particular, we also have that
(x,y)\in\sim_{\beta}^{(k)}
.
By transitivity,
x\in\sim_{\beta}^{(k)}z
, and
k
is the largest number such that
x\in\sim_{\beta}^{(k)}z
.
Therefore,
d(x,z)=2^{-k}=\max(d(x,y),d(y,z))

The only remaining case is symmetric.
∎

To prove
Lemma

24
, we first prove that substitution in Salomaa systems is well-behaved w.r.t. the operator used in the definition of the relation refinement chain on
(\mathtt{Exp},\partial)
.

Lemma 66
.

Let
(X,\tau:X\to\mathtt{Exp}(X))
be a Salomaa system and let
R\subseteq\mathtt{Exp}\times\mathtt{Exp}
be a congruence.
Consider two maps
m,n:X\to\mathtt{Exp}
satisfying
(m(x),n(x))\in R
for all
x\in X
. Then,

((m^{\#}\circ\tau)(x),(n^{\#}\circ\tau)(x))\in\Phi_{\partial}(R)

for all
x\in X
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that for all
x\in X
,

\tau(x)=\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I_{\alpha}^{x}}{{p}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{i}\;{{\oplus}}\;\bigoplus_{j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}}{{r}}_{j}\cdot\mathsf{g}_{j}x_{j}\right)

For any
x\in X
and
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
,
I_{\alpha}^{x}
and
J_{\alpha}^{x}
are disjoint index sets such that

•

\{{{p}}_{i}\}_{i\in I_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
\{{{r}}_{j}\}_{j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}}
are indexed collections of probabilities satisfying

\sum_{i\in I_{\alpha}^{x}}{{p}}_{i}+\sum_{j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}}{{r}}_{j}={{1}}

•

\{\mathsf{f}_{i}\}_{i\in I_{\alpha}^{x}}
and
\{\mathsf{g}_{j}\}_{j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}}
are indexed collections of elements of
\mathtt{Exp}
. Moreover, since
(X,\tau)
is Salomaa, we have that
\mathsf{E}\left(\mathsf{g}_{j}\right)=0
for all
j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}
. Because of
Lemma

44
we have that
\partial(\mathsf{g}_{j})_{\alpha}(\checkmark)={{0}}
for all for all
j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}
.

•

\{x_{j}\}_{j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}}
is an indexed collection of indeterminates (elements of
X
).

Observe that for all
x\in X
,

\displaystyle(m^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)

\displaystyle=\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I_{\alpha}^{x}}{{p}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{i}\;{{\oplus}}\;\bigoplus_{j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}}{{r}}_{j}\cdot\mathsf{g}_{j}\mathbin{;}m(x_{j})\right)

\displaystyle(n^{\#}\circ\tau)(x)

\displaystyle=\bbigplus\limits_{\alpha\in\mathsf{At}}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in I_{\alpha}^{x}}{{p}}_{i}\cdot\mathsf{f}_{i}\;{{\oplus}}\;\bigoplus_{j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}}{{r}}_{j}\cdot\mathsf{g}_{j}\mathbin{;}n(x_{j})\right)

For the rest of the argument, we use
Lemma

38
.
Pick an arbitrary
x\in X
and
\alpha\in\mathsf{At}
.

1.

For

, we have that

\displaystyle\partial((f^{\#}\circ m)(x))_{\alpha}(o)

\displaystyle\quad\partial((f^{\#}\circ m)(x))_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{a}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I_{\alpha}^{x}}{{p}}_{i}\partial(\mathsf{f}_{i})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{a}\}\times Q]+\sum_{j\in J_{\alpha}^{x}}{{r}}_{j}\partial(\mathsf{g_{j}}\mathbin{;}m(x_{j}))_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{a}\}\times Q]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I_{\alpha}^{x}}{{p}}_{i}\partial(\mathsf{f}_{i})_{\alpha}[\{\mathsf{a}\}\times Q]

(

Proof.

Observe that all elements of the relation refinement chain are congruences.
This allows us to use
Lemma

66
.
Recall that given
e,f\in\mathtt{Exp}^{n}
such that
e=(\mathsf{e}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{e}_{n})
and
f=(\mathsf{f}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{f}_{n})
, we have

d((\mathsf{e}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{e}_{n}),(\mathsf{f}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{f}_{n}))=\max\{d(\mathsf{e}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{1}),\dots,d(\mathsf{e}_{n},\mathsf{f}_{n})\}

As before, we slightly abuse notation and also write
e,f:X\to\mathtt{Exp}
for functions
e(x_{k})=\mathsf{e}_{k}
and
f(x_{k})=\mathsf{f}_{k}
for
1\leq k\leq n
.

Assume that
d((\mathsf{e}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{e}_{n}),(\mathsf{f}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{f}_{n}))\leq 2^{-m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
By definition of the product metric,
2^{-m}\geq\max\{d(\mathsf{e}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{1}),\dots,d(\mathsf{e}_{n},\mathsf{f}_{n})\}
, so
\mathsf{e}_{i}\sim_{\partial}^{(m)}\mathsf{f}_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
By
Lemma

66
,
((e^{\#}\circ\tau)(x_{i}),(f^{\#}\circ\tau)(x_{i}))\in\Phi_{\partial}\left(\sim_{\partial}^{(m)}\right)={\sim_{\partial}^{(m+1)}}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
Therefore,
d((e\circ\tau)(x_{i}),(f\circ\tau)(x_{i}))\leq 2^{-(m+1)}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
It follows that

d(\bar{\tau}(\mathsf{e}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{e}_{n}),\bar{\tau}(\mathsf{f}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{f}_{n}))\leq 2^{-(m+1)}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2^{m}}\right)

It follows that
\bar{\tau}
is
\frac{1}{2}
-Lipschitz.
∎
